2026-11-12T14:38 | owner:Arthur Ryzhak | decision | owner typed at about 14:36: "my take on the limit-exhaustion crash hazard is that it guarantees to let the cached context for all the agents and for the session to expire. if you wait to continue on limit reset - chances are the limit reset won't come in an hour (which is the cache TTL and cannot be configured). and if you re-login to get a new limit, the cache is identity-tied and you evict it anyway. this means that for every conversation - main or subagent - that must carry on through the limit exhaustion crash, we must now burn a big chunk of this new limit on paying the cost again." and then: "which means that resumption must be cache-and-token-spend-aware. and token spend and subagent launches and models choices token-limit-informed."

2026-11-12T17:01 | owner:Arthur Ryzhak | decision | round, settled: an agent's cost has three parts, output (tokens generated), intake (tokens entering the cache) and carry (the whole context read back at every call); the repeated intake of the whole context after the cache expired is called a cold start; context cost is intake plus carry
2026-11-12T17:03 | owner:Arthur Ryzhak | decision | round, names of the three per-token prices: not chosen; owner's replies: "the mapping is not one-to-one, is it? cache read/writes do not map cleanly on carry and intake, do they?" and "doesn't cached read scales factorially with conversation lifetime?"; the lead answers: carry is exactly the cache reads; intake is exactly the cache writes only when defined as every token entering the cache (new material, the agent's own earlier output, the whole context at a cold start), its price having two values by cache life; total carry grows as the triangular sum of the context over calls, quadratic in the agent's life, not factorial; the question is put again
2026-11-12T17:04 | owner:Arthur Ryzhak | decision | round, settled: the three per-token prices of a model are named after the parts: output price, intake price (one value per cache life, 5 minutes and 1 hour) and carry price; intake is every token entering the cache (new material, the agent's own earlier output, the whole context at a cold start), so intake equals the cache writes and carry the cache reads

Each implementer has its own worktree and branch; what orders two units is (1) a semantic dependency, one calling or testing what the other introduces, (2) nothing else by nature; overlapping hunks are a merge cost paid at the rebase and a delta re-review, never an order. The lead applied the convention against parallel agents writing one file (written for agents in one tree) to units on separate branches, against the dev-cycle rule that two units touching one file are the integrator's merge and are never serialized for it. From now a unit is held behind another only for a semantic dependency, a rule that names the case (a caller after its callee is integrated; integration one unit at a time; the quality review after the spec PASS at the final sha), the machine, or the window. Edges re-read: raw-lines-and-node-events and joiner-asks-before-handle start on the frozen heads of all-node (69221d8) and all-net-wire (tree of 1e45536) stacked, not after their merges; an early quality review runs beside the spec review on a unit's first head and both re-reviews run beside each other after a fix, the prescribed order kept at the final sha; all of it launches as the window allows.
